    "text": "Good question. Yes \u2014 and the approach is simpler than you might think, because your Kafka messaging already decouples the parent from the child's physical location. The parent doesn't care where the child runs \u2014 it only cares that a message arrives on the parent's responses topic.\n\nHere's the practical path, working from what `SpawnAgentAction` currently does.\n\n## What SpawnAgentAction does today (the steps that matter)\n\nLooking at your code, the flow is:\n\n1. Extract config (agent_type, role, client_id)\n2. Generate agent ID/name\n3. Create subtree tracking\n4. Get agent definition from DB\n5. Create agent in DB\n6. Setup Kafka topics for child\n7. **Call `spawnAgentKubernetesJobFromDefinition` \u2192 creates K8s Job locally**\n8. Pre-register awaited request\n9. Wait 5 seconds for pod startup\n10. Send init message to child's requests topic\n11. Return result with await\n\nSteps 1-6 and 8-11 don't care where the child runs. Only step 7 touches the local K8s API. So the plan is: keep `spawn_agent` unchanged, and create a `dispatch_agent` action that does steps 1-6 and 8-11 identically but replaces step 7 with a Kafka message to a dispatch topic.\n\n## What you'd need\n\n**Three things:**\n\n**1. Shared Kafka access.** The existing Kafka cluster needs to be reachable from cluster B. The simplest approach: expose it via a LoadBalancer or NodePort with no auth changes initially (you're on a private network anyway). You'd add an external listener to your Strimzi/Kafka config. The internal listener stays as-is, so nothing in cluster A changes.\n\n**2. A `dispatch_agent` action in spawn_actions.go.** This reuses almost everything from `SpawnAgentAction` but instead of calling `spawnAgentKubernetesJobFromDefinition`, it publishes a dispatch request to `system.dispatch.requests`. The message contains everything the remote cluster needs to create the Job: agent definition details, image/tag, env vars, topic names.\n\n**3. A small dispatcher service running in cluster B.** It consumes from `system.dispatch.requests`, creates local K8s Jobs, and confirms back on `system.dispatch.responses`. This is a lightweight Go service \u2014 maybe 200-300 lines \u2014 that uses the same K8s Job creation code you already have in `spawnAgentKubernetesJobFromDefinition`.\n\n## How it fits together\n\n```\nCluster A (existing)                    Cluster B (new)\n\u250c\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2510                \u250c\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2510\n\u2502 Parent agent         \u2502                \u2502 Dispatcher service   \u2502\n\u2502   workflow step:     \u2502                \u2502   consumes from      \u2502\n\u2502   dispatch_agent     \u2502                \u2502   system.dispatch \u2502\u2500\u2500\u2500Kafka\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2502  Creates K8s Job     \u2502\n\u2502  .requests           \u2502  (shared)      \u2502  locally             \u2502\n\u2502        \u2502             \u2502                \u2502        \u2502             \u2502\n\u2502  Pre-register        \u2502                \u2502  Child agent starts  \u2502\n\u2502 receives     \u2502\u25c4\u2500\u2500Kafka\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2502  (via shared Kafka)  \u2502\n\u2502  response as normal  \u2502                \u2502                      \u2502\n\u2514\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2518                \u2514\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2518\n```\n\nThe parent in cluster A doesn't know or care that the child is in cluster B. It sent an init message to a Kafka topic, and it got a response back on its responses topic. From the workflow engine's perspective, nothing changed.\n\n## The dispatch_agent action\n\nThis would live alongside `SpawnAgentAction` in `spawn_actions.go`. The key difference is just a few lines \u2014 instead of calling the K8s API, it publishes a message:\n\n```go\n// DispatchAgentAction dispatches agent creation to a remote cluster\n// Reuses all the same setup as SpawnAgentAction but sends a \n// dispatch message instead of creating a local K8s Job\nfunc DispatchAgentAction(ctx context.Context, params ActionParams) (interface{}, error) {\n    // Steps 1-6 are identical to SpawnAgentAction\n    agentType, role, clientID, _, err := extractSpawnConfiguration(params)\n    if err != nil {\n        return nil, fmt.Errorf(\"configuration extraction failed: %w\", err)\n    }\n\n    agentID := uuid.New().String()\n    agentName := GenerateAgentName(agentType, role)\n    subtreeInfo := createSubtreeInfo(agentID, agentName, agentType, \n        params.ExecutionContext.FromAgentID)\n\n    agentDef, err := getAgentDefinition(ctx, params.DB, agentType, params.Logger)\n    if err != nil {\n        return nil, fmt.Errorf(\"failed to get agent definition: %w\", err)\n    }\n\n    if err := createAgentInDBFromDefinition(ctx, params, agentID, agentDef, clientID); err != nil {\n        return nil, fmt.Errorf(\"failed to create agent in DB: %w\", err)\n    }\n\n    childRequestsTopic, childResponsesTopic, parentResponsesTopic, \n        stableIdentity, err := setupAgentTopics(ctx, params, agentType, agentID)\n    if err != nil {\n        return nil, fmt.Errorf(\"failed to setup topics: %w\", err)\n    }\n\n    // Step 7 DIFFERS: publish dispatch request instead of K8s Job\n    targetCluster := \"default\"\n    if cluster, ok := params.StepConfig.Config[\"cluster\"].(string); ok {\n        targetCluster = cluster\n    }\n\n    dispatchRequest := map[string]interface{}{\n        \"agent_id\":               agentID,\n        \"agent_type\":             agentType,\n        \"agent_name\":             agentName,\n        \"role\":                   role,\n        \"client_id\":              clientID,\n        \"image_repository\":       agentDef.ImageRepository,\n        \"image_tag\":              agentDef.ImageTag,\n        \"command\":                agentDef.Command,\n        \"resources\":              agentDef.Resources,\n        \"requests_topic\":         childRequestsTopic,\n        \"responses_topic\":        childResponsesTopic,\n        \"parent_responses_topic\": parentResponsesTopic,\n        \"target_cluster\":         targetCluster,\n        \"env_vars\":               agentDef.EnvVars,\n    }\n\n    dispatchJSON, _ := json.Marshal(dispatchRequest)\n    headers := map[string]string{\n        \"correlation_id\":   params.ExecutionContext.CorrelationID,\n        \"orchestration_id\": params.ExecutionContext.OrchestrationID,\n        \"agent_id\":         agentID,\n        \"agent_type\":       agentType,\n        \"target_cluster\":   targetCluster,\n    }\n\n    params.Producer.Produce(ctx, \"system.dispatch.requests\", headers,\n        []byte(agentID), dispatchJSON)\n\n    params.Logger.Info(\"Dispatched agent to remote cluster\",\n        zap.String(\"agent_id\", agentID),\n        zap.String(\"agent_type\", agentType),\n        zap.String(\"target_cluster\", targetCluster),\n        zap.String(\"requests_topic\", childRequestsTopic))\n\n    // Steps 8-11 are identical to SpawnAgentAction\n    initRequestID := uuid.New().String()\n\n    if params.DB != nil {\n        if err := preRegisterAwaitedRequest(ctx, params, initRequestID, agentID, \n            agentType, childRequestsTopic, parentResponsesTopic); err != nil {\n            params.Logger.Warn(\"Failed to pre-register awaited request\",\n                zap.Error(err))\n        }\n    }\n\n    // Slightly longer wait for remote \u2014 network latency + scheduling\n    time.Sleep(10 * time.Second)\n\n    if err := sendInitializationMessage(ctx, params, agentID, agentName, agentType, \n        role, initRequestID, childRequestsTopic, parentResponsesTopic); err != nil {\n        params.Logger.Error(\"Failed to send initialization message\",\n            zap.Error(err))\n    }\n\n    // Return same result structure as SpawnAgentAction\n    return buildSpawnResult(agentID, agentName, agentType, role, \n        childRequestsTopic, childResponsesTopic, subtreeInfo), nil\n}\n```\n\n## The dispatcher service in cluster B\n\nThis is a simple consumer that reads dispatch requests and creates local K8s Jobs. It would reuse your existing `spawnAgentKubernetesJobFromDefinition` function (or a version of it). The dispatcher needs:\n\n- Access to the shared Kafka cluster\n- Access to the local K8s API in cluster B\n- The same container images available (your image registry needs to be accessible from both clusters \u2014 since you're using `docker.io/aqls/agent-chassis`, this already works)\n\nThe dispatcher doesn't need access to Postgres \u2014 the parent already created the DB records. It just needs to create the K8s Job with the right env vars.\n\n## Using it in a workflow\n\nA workflow step would look almost identical to a spawn \u2014 just a different action name and an optional cluster hint:\n\n```json\n{\n    \"spawn_researcher\": {\n        \"action\": \"dispatch_agent\",\n        \"config\": {\n            \"agent_type\": \"content-researcher\",\n            \"role\": \"researcher\",\n            \"cluster\": \"cluster-b\"\n        },\n        \"description\": \"Dispatch research agent to cluster B\",\n        \"next_step\": \"call_researcher\"\n    }\n}\n```\n\nIf you omit `cluster`, it defaults. You could even make the dispatcher smart enough to pick a cluster based on load, but that's later.\n\n## What about Postgres access from cluster B?\n\nThe spawned agent in cluster B needs to read its workflow from Postgres and write orchestration state. Two options, in order of simplicity:\n\n**Option A (simplest start):** Expose your existing Postgres via a LoadBalancer on your private network. The agent's `DATABASE_URL` env var already gets set in `spawnAgentKubernetesJobFromDefinition` \u2014 you'd just need the dispatcher to substitute the external hostname instead of the `.svc.cluster.local` address. The dispatcher knows its own cluster context, so it can rewrite that env var before creating the Job.\n\n**Option B (slightly more work):** Run a PgBouncer in cluster B that proxies to the primary Postgres in cluster A. Agents in cluster B connect to their local PgBouncer. This gives you connection pooling at the remote end and a single point to manage the cross-cluster DB connection.\n\n## What doesn't change\n\n- `SpawnAgentAction` \u2014 untouched, still works for local spawning\n- Kafka topic naming \u2014 identical\n- Message format \u2014 identical\n- Workflow engine \u2014 the `dispatch_agent` action returns the same result structure, so `call_agent`, `aggregate_data`, `complete_workflow` all work as before\n- The spawned agent's code \u2014 it doesn't know it was dispatched remotely; it just reads from its topics and responds\n\n## The order of work\n\n1. Expose Kafka externally (LoadBalancer on your Strimzi cluster \u2014 a config change, no code)\n2. Expose Postgres externally (or set up PgBouncer in cluster B pointing at cluster A's Postgres)\n3. Register `DispatchAgentAction` in your action registry alongside the existing `SpawnAgentAction`\n4. Write the dispatcher service for cluster B (small \u2014 it's mostly the K8s Job creation code you already have, wrapped in a Kafka consumer)\n5. Deploy the dispatcher to cluster B along with the agent-chassis image\n6. Test with a single workflow that dispatches one agent remotely\n\nThe latency hit will be Kafka round-trip between clusters (probably 10-50ms depending on your network) plus the remote Job scheduling time. For your workloads where agents run for 30 seconds to several minutes, that's negligible. The 5-second sleep in `SpawnAgentAction` already accounts for more latency than cross-cluster Kafka would add \u2014 bumping it to 10 seconds for remote dispatch gives plenty of margin.\n\nWould you like me to look at the action registry code to see exactly how to register the new `dispatch_agent` action, or focus on the dispatcher service for cluster B first?",

      "file_text": "// FILE: platform/orchestration/actions/dispatch_actions.go\n//\n// DispatchAgentAction provides a remote-cluster alternative to SpawnAgentAction.\n// It reuses the same helper functions (extractSpawnConfiguration, setupAgentTopics,\n// createAgentInDBFromDefinition, sendInitializationMessage, buildSpawnResult, etc.)\n// but instead of calling spawnAgentKubernetesJobFromDefinition (which talks to the\n// local K8s API), it publishes a dispatch request to a Kafka topic.\n//\n// A lightweight dispatcher service running in the target cluster consumes these\n// messages and creates local K8s Jobs. The parent agent doesn't know or care\n// where the child runs \u2014 it communicates through the same Kafka topics either way.\n//\n// Usage in workflows:\n//\n//\t\"spawn_researcher\": {\n//\t    \"action\": \"dispatch_agent\",\n//\t    \"config\": {\n//\t        \"agent_type\": \"content-researcher\",\n//\t        \"role\": \"researcher\",\n//\t        \"target_cluster\": \"cluster-b\"\n//\t    },\n//\t    \"next_step\": \"call_researcher\"\n//\t}\n//\n// If \"target_cluster\" is omitted, it defaults to \"default\".\n// All other config fields (agent_type, role, etc.) are identical to spawn_agent.\n\npackage actions\n\nimport (\n\t\"context\"\n\t\"encoding/json\"\n\t\"fmt\"\n\t\"os\"\n\t\"time\"\n\n\t\"github.com/google/uuid\"\n\t\"go.uber.org/zap\"\n)\n\n// DispatchRequest is the message payload sent to the dispatch topic.\n// The remote dispatcher uses this to create a K8s Job in its local cluster.\ntype DispatchRequest struct {\n\t// Agent identity\n\tAgentID   string `json:\"agent_id\"`\n\tAgentType string `json:\"agent_type\"`\n\tAgentName string `json:\"agent_name\"`\n\tRole      string `json:\"role\"`\n\tClientID  string `json:\"client_id\"`\n\n\t// Container spec \u2014 from agent_definitions\n\tImageRepository string          `json:\"image_repository\"`\n\tImageTag        string          `json:\"image_tag\"`\n\tCommand         []string        `json:\"command\"`\n\tResources       json.RawMessage `json:\"resources\"`\n\tHealthConfig    json.RawMessage `json:\"health_config\"`\n\tEnvVars         json.RawMessage `json:\"env_vars\"`\n\tCategory        string          `json:\"category\"`\n\n\t// Kafka topics for the spawned agent\n\tRequestsTopic        string `json:\"requests_topic\"`\n\tResponsesTopic       string `json:\"responses_topic\"`\n\tParentResponsesTopic string `json:\"parent_responses_topic\"`\n\n\t// Target cluster identifier\n\tTargetCluster string `json:\"target_cluster\"`\n\n\t// Infrastructure config \u2014 the dispatcher will use these to build\n\t// the DATABASE_URL and KAFKA_BROKERS env vars for the spawned agent.\n\t// If empty, the dispatcher uses its own local defaults.\n\tKafkaBrokers   string `json:\"kafka_brokers,omitempty\"`\n\tDatabaseHost   string `json:\"database_host,omitempty\"`\n\tDatabasePort   string `json:\"database_port,omitempty\"`\n\tDatabaseUser   string `json:\"database_user,omitempty\"`\n\tDatabaseName   string `json:\"database_name,omitempty\"`\n\tTemplatesDBHost string `json:\"templates_db_host,omitempty\"`\n\tTemplatesDBPort string `json:\"templates_db_port,omitempty\"`\n\tTemplatesDBUser string `json:\"templates_db_user,omitempty\"`\n\tTemplatesDBName string `json:\"templates_db_name,omitempty\"`\n\n\t// Timestamp for dispatch tracking\n\tDispatchedAt string `json:\"dispatched_at\"`\n}\n\nconst (\n\t// DispatchRequestsTopic is the topic the dispatcher listens on\n\tDispatchRequestsTopic = \"system.dispatch.requests\"\n\n\t// DispatchResponsesTopic is where the dispatcher confirms job creation\n\tDispatchResponsesTopic = \"system.dispatch.responses\"\n\n\t// DefaultRemoteStartupWait is longer than local (5s) to account for\n\t// cross-cluster Kafka latency + remote K8s scheduling\n\tDefaultRemoteStartupWait = 12 * time.Second\n\n\t// DefaultRemoteConsumerWait is the second wait after init message\n\tDefaultRemoteConsumerWait = 8 * time.Second\n)\n\n// DispatchAgentAction dispatches agent creation to a remote cluster via Kafka.\n//\n// Steps 1-6 and 8-11 are identical to SpawnAgentAction.\n// Step 7 replaces the local K8s Job call with a Kafka dispatch message.\n//\n// NOTE: variable names are intentionally kept identical to SpawnAgentAction\n// so the two functions stay in sync during maintenance.\nfunc DispatchAgentAction(ctx context.Context, params ActionParams) (interface{}, error) {\n\tparams.Logger.Info(\"DispatchAgentAction starting\",\n\t\tzap.Any(\"config\", params.StepConfig),\n\t\tzap.Any(\"headers\", params.Headers))\n\n\t// --- Steps 1-6: identical to SpawnAgentAction ---\n\n\t// 1. Validate and extract configuration\n\tagentType, role, clientID, _, err := extractSpawnConfiguration(params)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"configuration extraction failed in dispatch actions: %w\", err)\n\t}\n\n\t// 2. Generate agent identities\n\tagentID := uuid.New().String()\n\tagentName := GenerateAgentName(agentType, role)\n\n\t// 3. Create agent hierarchy tracking\n\tsubtreeInfo := createSubtreeInfo(agentID, agentName, agentType, params.ExecutionContext.FromAgentID)\n\n\t// 4. Get agent definition from database\n\tagentDef, err := getAgentDefinition(ctx, params.DB, agentType, params.Logger)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\tparams.Logger.Error(\"Failed to get agent definition\",\n\t\t\tzap.String(\"agent_type\", agentType),\n\t\t\tzap.Error(err))\n\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"failed to get agent definition: %w\", err)\n\t}\n\n\t// 5. Create agent in database (in the local/primary DB \u2014 same as spawn)\n\tif err := createAgentInDBFromDefinition(ctx, params, agentID, agentDef, clientID); err != nil {\n\t\tparams.Logger.Error(\"Failed to create agent in database\",\n\t\t\tzap.String(\"agent_id\", agentID),\n\t\t\tzap.Error(err))\n\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"failed to create agent in DB: %w\", err)\n\t}\n\n\t// 6. Setup topic configuration (creates Kafka topics on the shared cluster)\n\tchildRequestsTopic, childResponsesTopic, parentResponsesTopic, stableIdentity, err := setupAgentTopics(ctx, params, agentType, agentID)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\tparams.Logger.Error(\"Failed to setup topics\",\n\t\t\tzap.String(\"agent_id\", agentID),\n\t\t\tzap.Error(err))\n\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"failed to setup topics: %w\", err)\n\t}\n\n\t// --- Step 7: DISPATCH instead of local K8s Job ---\n\n\ttargetCluster := \"default\"\n\tif cluster, ok := params.StepConfig.Config[\"target_cluster\"].(string); ok && cluster != \"\" {\n\t\ttargetCluster = cluster\n\t}\n\n\tdispatchReq := DispatchRequest{\n\t\tAgentID:              agentID,\n\t\tAgentType:            agentType,\n\t\tAgentName:            agentName,\n\t\tRole:                 role,\n\t\tClientID:             clientID,\n\t\tImageRepository:      agentDef.ImageRepository,\n\t\tImageTag:             agentDef.ImageTag,\n\t\tCommand:              agentDef.Command,\n\t\tResources:            agentDef.Resources,\n\t\tHealthConfig:         agentDef.HealthConfig,\n\t\tEnvVars:              agentDef.EnvVars,\n\t\tCategory:             agentDef.Category,\n\t\tRequestsTopic:        childRequestsTopic,\n\t\tResponsesTopic:       childResponsesTopic,\n\t\tParentResponsesTopic: parentResponsesTopic,\n\t\tTargetCluster:        targetCluster,\n\t\t// Pass infra config so the dispatcher can build env vars for the agent.\n\t\t// The dispatcher may override these with its own local values.\n\t\tKafkaBrokers:    os.Getenv(\"KAFKA_BROKERS\"),\n\t\tDatabaseHost:    os.Getenv(\"SERVICE_INFRASTRUCTURE_CLIENTS_DATABASE_HOST\"),\n\t\tDatabasePort:    os.Getenv(\"SERVICE_INFRASTRUCTURE_CLIENTS_DATABASE_PORT\"),\n\t\tDatabaseUser:    os.Getenv(\"SERVICE_INFRASTRUCTURE_CLIENTS_DATABASE_USER\"),\n\t\tDatabaseName:    os.Getenv(\"SERVICE_INFRASTRUCTURE_CLIENTS_DATABASE_DB_NAME\"),\n\t\tTemplatesDBHost: os.Getenv(\"SERVICE_INFRASTRUCTURE_TEMPLATES_DATABASE_HOST\"),\n\t\tTemplatesDBPort: os.Getenv(\"SERVICE_INFRASTRUCTURE_TEMPLATES_DATABASE_PORT\"),\n\t\tTemplatesDBUser: os.Getenv(\"SERVICE_INFRASTRUCTURE_TEMPLATES_DATABASE_USER\"),\n\t\tTemplatesDBName: os.Getenv(\"SERVICE_INFRASTRUCTURE_TEMPLATES_DATABASE_DB_NAME\"),\n\t\tDispatchedAt:    time.Now().UTC().Format(time.RFC3339),\n\t}\n\n\tdispatchJSON, err := json.Marshal(dispatchReq)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"failed to marshal dispatch request: %w\", err)\n\t}\n\n\theaders := map[string]string{\n\t\t\"correlation_id\":   params.ExecutionContext.CorrelationID,\n\t\t\"orchestration_id\": params.ExecutionContext.OrchestrationID,\n\t\t\"agent_id\":         agentID,\n\t\t\"agent_type\":       agentType,\n\t\t\"target_cluster\":   targetCluster,\n\t\t\"message_type\":     \"dispatch_request\",\n\t}\n\n\tparams.Producer.Produce(ctx, DispatchRequestsTopic, headers,\n\t\t[]byte(agentID), dispatchJSON)\n\n\tparams.Logger.Info(\"Dispatched agent to remote cluster\",\n\t\tzap.String(\"agent_id\", agentID),\n\t\tzap.String(\"agent_type\", agentType),\n\t\tzap.String(\"target_cluster\", targetCluster),\n\t\tzap.String(\"dispatch_topic\", DispatchRequestsTopic),\n\t\tzap.String(\"requests_topic\", childRequestsTopic),\n\t\tzap.String(\"responses_topic\", childResponsesTopic),\n\t\tzap.String(\"parent_responses_topic\", parentResponsesTopic))\n\n\t// --- Steps 8-11: identical to SpawnAgentAction (with longer waits) ---\n\n\t// 8. Generate the initialization request ID\n\tinitRequestID := uuid.New().String()\n\n\t// 9. Pre-register the awaited request\n\tif params.DB != nil {\n\t\tif err := preRegisterAwaitedRequest(ctx, params, initRequestID, agentID, agentType,\n\t\t\tchildRequestsTopic, parentResponsesTopic); err != nil {\n\t\t\tparams.Logger.Warn(\"Failed to pre-register awaited request - response matching may fail\",\n\t\t\t\tzap.String(\"request_id\", initRequestID),\n\t\t\t\tzap.Error(err))\n\t\t} else {\n\t\t\tparams.Logger.Info(\"Pre-registered awaited request before sending init message\",\n\t\t\t\tzap.String(\"request_id\", initRequestID),\n\t\t\t\tzap.String(\"orchestration_id\", params.ExecutionContext.OrchestrationID))\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\t// 10. Wait for remote pod startup\n\t// Longer than local (5s) because: Kafka cross-cluster latency + dispatcher\n\t// processing + remote K8s scheduling + image pull (if not cached)\n\tstartupWait := DefaultRemoteStartupWait\n\tif customWait, ok := params.StepConfig.Config[\"startup_wait_seconds\"].(float64); ok && customWait > 0 {\n\t\tstartupWait = time.Duration(customWait) * time.Second\n\t}\n\n\tparams.Logger.Info(\"Waiting for remote pod startup...\",\n\t\tzap.String(\"agent_id\", agentID),\n\t\tzap.String(\"agent_type\", agentType),\n\t\tzap.String(\"target_cluster\", targetCluster),\n\t\tzap.Duration(\"wait\", startupWait))\n\n\ttime.Sleep(startupWait)\n\n\t// 11. Send initialization message\n\tif err := sendInitializationMessage(ctx, params, agentID, agentName, agentType, role,\n\t\tinitRequestID, childRequestsTopic, parentResponsesTopic); err != nil {\n\t\tparams.Logger.Error(\"Failed to send initialization message\",\n\t\t\tzap.String(\"agent_id\", agentID),\n\t\t\tzap.Error(err))\n\t\t// Don't fail \u2014 agent might still come up and process it\n\t}\n\n\t// 12. Wait for Kafka consumers to start on the remote agent\n\tconsumerWait := DefaultRemoteConsumerWait\n\tif customWait, ok := params.StepConfig.Config[\"consumer_wait_seconds\"].(float64); ok && customWait > 0 {\n\t\tconsumerWait = time.Duration(customWait) * time.Second\n\t}\n\n\tparams.Logger.Info(\"Initialization message sent, waiting for remote Kafka consumers...\",\n\t\tzap.String(\"agent_id\", agentID),\n\t\tzap.String(\"agent_type\", agentType),\n\t\tzap.Duration(\"wait\", consumerWait))\n\n\ttime.Sleep(consumerWait)\n\n\t// 13. Build and return result \u2014 same structure as SpawnAgentAction\n\treturn buildSpawnResult(agentID, agentName, agentType, role, initRequestID,\n\t\tchildRequestsTopic, childResponsesTopic, parentResponsesTopic,\n\t\tstableIdentity, subtreeInfo), nil\n}\n",

      "file_text": "// FILE: cmd/dispatcher/main.go\n//\n// Remote Agent Dispatcher\n//\n// A lightweight service that runs in each remote cluster.\n// It consumes agent dispatch requests from Kafka (system.dispatch.requests)\n// and creates local Kubernetes Jobs.\n//\n// The dispatcher doesn't need access to PostgreSQL \u2014 the parent agent in the\n// originating cluster already created all DB records. This service only needs:\n//   - Kafka access (shared cluster or local federated broker)\n//   - Local Kubernetes API access (to create Jobs)\n//   - Access to the same container images (docker.io/aqls/agent-chassis)\n//\n// Configuration is via environment variables:\n//   KAFKA_BROKERS          \u2014 comma-separated broker list\n//   CLUSTER_ID             \u2014 identifier for this cluster (e.g. \"cluster-b\")\n//   NAMESPACE              \u2014 K8s namespace for Jobs (default: \"ai-persona-system\")\n//   CONSUMER_GROUP         \u2014 Kafka consumer group (default: \"dispatcher-{CLUSTER_ID}\")\n//   DISPATCH_TOPIC         \u2014 topic to consume (default: \"system.dispatch.requests\")\n//   DISPATCH_RESPONSE_TOPIC \u2014 topic for confirmations (default: \"system.dispatch.responses\")\n//\n// Infrastructure overrides (if the remote cluster should use different DB/Kafka\n// endpoints than what the originating cluster sent in the dispatch message):\n// `json:\"templates_db_name,omitempty\"`\n\tDispatchedAt         string          `json:\"dispatched_at\"`\n}\n\n// ResourceSpec matches the agent_definitions.resources JSON structure\ntype ResourceSpec struct {\n\tRequests map[string]string `json:\"requests\"`\n\tLimits   map[string]string `json:\"limits\"`\n}\n\n// HealthSpec matches the agent_definitions.health_config JSON structure\ntype environment\n\tkafkaBrokers := getEnvOrDefault(\"KAFKA_BROKERS\",\n\t\t\"personae-kafka-cluster-kafka-bootstrap.kafka.svc.cluster.local:9092\")\n\tclusterID := getEnvOrDefault(\"CLUSTER_ID\", 1)\n\tsignal.Notify(sigCh, syscall.SIGINT, syscall.SIGTERM)\n\tgo func() {\n\t\tsig := <-sigCh\n\t\tlogger.Info(\"Received shutdown signal\", zap.String(\"signal\", sig.String()))\n\t\tcancel()\n\t}()\n\n\tlogger.Info(\"Dispatcher ready, consuming from dispatch topic\")\n\n\t// Main consume loop\n\tfor {\n\t\tmsg, err := reader.ReadMessage(ctx)\n\t\tif err != nil targetCluster != clusterID && targetCluster != \"any\" {\n\t\t\tlogger.Debug(\"Message not for this cluster, skipping\",\n\t\t\t\tzap.String(\"target_cluster\", targetCluster),\n\t\t\t\tzap.String(\"my_cluster\", clusterID))\n\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// Parse dispatch request\n\t\tvar req DispatchRequest\n\t\tif err := json.Unmarshal(msg.Value, &req); err responseTopic,\n\t\t\tKey:   []byte(req.AgentID),\n\t\t\tValue: respJSON,\n\t\t\tHeaders: []kafka.Header{\n\t\t\t\t{Key: \"agent_id\", Value: []byte(req.AgentID)},\n\t\t\t\t{Key: \"agent_type\", \"message_type\", Value: []byte(\"dispatch_response\")},\n\t\t\t},\n\t\t})\n\t\tif writeErr != nil {\n\t\t\tlogger.Error(\"Failed to send dispatch response\", zap.Error(writeErr))\n\t\t}\n\t}\n}\n\n// createAgentJob creates a K8s Job in the local cluster.\n// This mirrors spawnAgentKubernetesJobFromDefinition from spawn_actions.go\n// but receives all parameters via the DispatchRequest instead of reading\n// from local environment and K8s API.\nfunc createAgentJob(ctx context.Context, clientset *kubernetes.Clientset, namespace, clusterID string, req *DispatchRequest, logger *zap.Logger) (string, error) {\n\tjobName := fmt.Sprintf(\"agent-%s-%s\", req.AgentType, req.AgentID[:8])\n\n\t// Check for existing job\n\texistingJob, err := clientset.BatchV1().Jobs(namespace).Get(ctx, jobName, metav1.GetOptions{})\n\tif err == nil {\n\t\tif existingJob.Status.Failed > 0 || existingJob.Status.Succeeded > 0 {\n\t\t\tlogger.Info(\"Deleting completed/failed job before recreating\",\n\t\t\t\tzap.String(\"job_name\", jobName))\n\t\t\tdeletePolicy := metav1.DeletePropagationForeground\n\t\t\t_ = clientset.BatchV1().Jobs(namespace).Delete(ctx, jobName, metav1.DeleteOptions{\n\t\t\t\tPropagationPolicy: &deletePolicy,\n\t\t\t})\n\t\t\ttime.Sleep(2 * time.Second)\n\t\t} else if existingJob.Status.Active > 0 {\n\t\t\tlogger.Info(\"Job already running\", zap.String(\"job_name\", jobName))\n\t\t\treturn jobName, nil\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\t// Parse specs from the dispatch request\n\tresources := parseResourceSpec(req.Resources)\n\thealthConfig := parseHealthSpec(req.HealthConfig)\n\tenvVars := parseEnvVarSpecs(req.EnvVars)\n\n\t// Resolve infrastructure endpoints.\n\t// Use overrides from this cluster's env if set, otherwise use what the\n\t// originating cluster sent in the dispatch message.\n\tkafkaBrokers := resolveConfig(\"OVERRIDE_KAFKA_BROKERS\", req.KafkaBrokers,\n\t\t\"personae-kafka-cluster-kafka-bootstrap.kafka.svc.cluster.local:9092\")\n\tdbHost := resolveConfig(\"OVERRIDE_DATABASE_HOST\", resolveConfig(\"OVERRIDE_TEMPLATES_DB_NAME\", req.TemplatesDBName, \"templates_db\")\n\tcoreManagerURL := resolveConfig(\"OVERRIDE_CORE_MANAGER_URL\", \"KAFKA_TOPICS\", Value: req.RequestsTopic},\n\t\t{Name: \"RESPONSES_TOPIC\", Value: req.ResponsesTopic},\n\t\t{Name: \"REQUESTS_TOPIC\", Value: req.RequestsTopic},\n\t\t{Name: \"PARENT_RESPONSES_TOPIC\", Value: req.ParentResponsesTopic},\n\t\t{Name: \"KAFKA_CONSUMER_GROUP\", Value: fmt.Sprintf(\"%s-group-%s\", req.AgentType, req.AgentID[:8])},\n\n\t\t// Infrastructure\n\t\t{Name: \"KAFKA_BROKERS\", Value: kafkaBrokers},\n\t\t{Name: \"SERVICE_INFRASTRUCTURE_KAFKA_BROKERS\", Value: kafkaBrokers},\n\t\t{Name: \"DATABASE_URL\", Value: dbURL},\n\t\t{Name: \"SERVICE_INFRASTRUCTURE_CLIENTS_DATABASE_USER\", Value: dbUser},\n\t\t{Name: \"SERVICE_INFRASTRUCTURE_CLIENTS_DATABASE_DB_NAME\", Value: dbName},\n\t\t{Name: personae-default-secrets  (ANTHROPIC_API_KEY)\n\tenvList = append(envList, []corev1.EnvVar{\n\t\tsecretEnvVar(\"CLIENTS_DB_PASSWORD\", \"personae-platform-secrets\",     "type": "text",
    "text": "Here's what each file does and the key decisions I made:\n\n**`dispatch_actions.go`** \u2014 drops into `platform/orchestration/actions/` alongside your existing `spawn_actions.go`. `DispatchAgentAction` calls the same helper functions as `SpawnAgentAction` \u2014 `extractSpawnConfiguration`, `setupAgentTopics`, `createAgentInDBFromDefinition`, `sendInitializationMessage`, `buildSpawnResult` \u2014 unchanged. The only difference is step 7: instead of calling `spawnAgentKubernetesJobFromDefinition`, it publishes a `DispatchRequest` to `system.dispatch.requests`. The return value is identical to `SpawnAgentAction`, so the coordinator, `call_agent`, and `complete_workflow` all work as before.\n\n**`cmd_dispatcher_main.go`** \u2014 standalone service for cluster B. Consumes from `system.dispatch.requests`, filters by `target_cluster` header, creates local K8s Jobs using the same Job spec structure as `spawnAgentKubernetesJobFromDefinition`. Key difference: infrastructure endpoints are resolved through a three-tier priority: local env override \u2192 dispatch message value \u2192 hardcoded defaults. This means cluster B can use its own PgBouncer address or Kafka endpoint without the parent agent needing to know about it.\n\n**`registry_patch.go`** \u2014 just a comment showing the one line to add to `GlobalActionRegistry`.\n\n**Things to note:**\n\n- I kept all variable names from the existing code unchanged \u2014 `childRequestsTopic`, `parentResponsesTopic`, `stableIdentity`, etc.\n- The `DispatchRequest` struct is defined in both files independently (no import dependency between the chassis and the dispatcher). If you later want to share it, you could extract it to a common package.\n- The dispatcher adds two labels to Jobs it creates: `spawned-by: dispatcher` and `dispatcher-cluster: cluster-b`, so you can distinguish locally-spawned from remotely-dispatched agents in `kubectl`.\n- The startup/consumer waits are configurable per-step via `startup_wait_seconds` and `consumer_wait_seconds` in the workflow config, with longer defaults than local spawn (12s and 8s vs 5s and 5s).\n- Cluster B needs the same K8s secrets (`personae-platform-secrets`, `personae-default-secrets`, `docker-hub-creds`) and ConfigMap (`personae-prod-config`). You'd copy these once during cluster setup.",
